\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}w\,|\,\left(v\in\mathrm{Sc}^{\mathbf{G}}(w)\right)\,\lor\,\left(\exists\tilde{v}\in\mathrm{Sc}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)\,\land\,\exists\tilde{w}\in\mathrm{Sc}^{\mathbf{G}}(w):\;\tilde{v}\mathrel{\hbox to13.4pt{\vbox to0.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower 1.95277pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 2.98 L 13.01 2.98}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{3.59995pt}{2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 0 C 3.51 0.28 1.11 1.11 -0.55 2.08 L -0.55 -2.08 C 1.11 -1.11 3.51 -0.28 4.98 0}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{9.40007pt}{2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 0 C 3.51 0.28 1.11 1.11 -0.55 2.08 L -0.55 -2.08 C 1.11 -1.11 3.51 -0.28 4.98 0}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\tilde{w}\in\mathbf{G}\right)\right\}.

Theorem H.8

(See
[
FM17
,
FM18
,
FM20
]
)
.

Let
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))
be a CDMG, then its acyclification
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V|\doit(J))

is a conditional
acyclic
directed mixed graph (CDAMG)
and for all subsets
A,B,C\subseteq J\cup V
we have the equivalence:

A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}B\given C\quad\iff\quad A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}B\given C\quad\iff\quad A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\emptyset))}^{\sigma}J\cup B\given C,

where the both right hand relations can, due their acyclicity, ignore the the extra conditions
v_{k}\notin\mathrm{Sc}^{\mathbf{G}}(v_{\pm k})

in the Definition
5.8

of
\sigma
-separation.

Appendix I
Proofs - Separoid Rules for Sigma-Separation

In the following let
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))
be a CDMG and
A,B,C,D\subseteq J\cup V

(not necessarily disjoint) subsets of nodes.

Since by Theorem
H.8

we have the equivalence:

A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}B\given C\quad\iff\quad A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}B\given C\quad\iff\quad A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\emptyset))}^{\sigma}J\cup B\given C,

we can restrict ourselves to
acyclic
graphs.
It is known that for acylic graphs without input nodes like
\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\emptyset))

the relation
\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\emptyset))}

is a (symmetric) separoid, see e.g.
[
Ric03
,
FM17
]
.
By the general theory in Appendix
A
Remark
A.15

we then know that
\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\emptyset))}

is a
\emptyset
-
\emptyset
-separoid, see Definition
A.4
.
By Theorem
A.16
we then know that the relation
\Perp_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}

is a
J
-
\emptyset
-separoid and thus satisfies all the separoid rules from Theorem
5.11
.
This already completes the proof.

Since we are interested in proving the global Markov property for (
acyclic
) causal Bayesian networks, we
will, for completeness sake, in the following give detailed proofs for all separoid rules again, by assuming, w.l.o.g. that
\mathbf{G}=\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V|\doit(J))
is acyclic.
For that recall that for a CDMG
\mathbf{G}
we say that

A
is
\sigma
-separated from
B
given
C
in
\mathbf{G}
, in symbols:

A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}}^{\sigma}B\given C,

if every walk from a node in
A
to a node in
J\cup B
(sic!) is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
.

For
acyclic
CDMGs, a walk
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
if it either contains a non-collider
(i.e. either an end node, fork, left/right chain) in
C
or a collider not in
C
.

We abbreviate the ternary relations in the following:
\displaystyle\Perp:=\Perp_{\mathbf{G}}^{\sigma}=\Perp_{\mathbf{G}^{\mathrm{acy}}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}
.

I.1
Core Separoid Rules for Sigma-Separation

Lemma I.1

(
\emptyset
-Extended Left Redundancy)
.

A\subseteq C\implies A\Perp B\given C.

Proof.

If
\pi
is a walk from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J\cup B
then its first end node is in
A
, so
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
A
.
∎

Lemma I.2

(
J
-Restricted Right Redundancy)
.

A\Perp\emptyset\given C\cup J\qquad\text{always holds.}

Proof.

If
\pi
is a walk from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J
then its last end node is in
C\cup J
, so
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
A
.
∎

Lemma I.3

(Left Decomposition)
.

A\cup D\Perp B\given C\implies D\Perp B\given C.

Proof.

If
\pi
is a walk from a node
v
in
D
to a node
w
in
J\cup B
, then
\pi
is a walk
from
A\cup D
to
J\cup B
, which by assumption is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
.
∎

Lemma I.4

(Right Decomposition)
.

A\Perp B\cup D\given C\implies A\Perp D\given C.

Proof.

If
\pi
is a walk from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J\cup D
, then
\pi
is a walk from

A
to
J\cup B\cup D
, which by assumption is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
.
∎

Lemma I.5

(
J
-Inverted Right Decomposition)
.

A\Perp B\given C\implies A\Perp J\cup B\given C.

Proof.

If
\pi
is a walk from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J\cup J\cup B
then
w\in J\cup B
.
If
w\in J\cup B
then by assumption
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
.
∎

Lemma I.6

(Left Weak Union)
.

A\cup D\Perp B\given C\implies A\Perp B\given D\cup C.

Proof.

Lets assume the contrary:
A\nPerp B\given D\cup C
. Then there exists a shortest
(D\cup C)
-
\sigma
-open walk
\pi

from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J\cup B
in
\mathbf{G}
.
Then every collider of
\pi
is in
D\cup C
and every non-collider of
\pi
is not in
D\cup C
.

If now
\pi
does not contain any node from
D\setminus C
then every collider of
\pi
lies in
C
.
This implies that
\pi
is
C
-
\sigma
-open,
which contradicts the assumption:
A\cup D\Perp B\given C
.

So we can assume now that
\pi
contains a node in
D\setminus C
.
Then consider the shortest sub-walk
\tilde{\pi}
in

\pi
from
w\in J\cup B
to a node
u\in D\setminus C
. This means that
\tilde{\pi}
does not contain any collider in
D\setminus C
,
so they are all in
C
. So
\tilde{\pi}
is
C
-
\sigma
-open walk from
A\cup D
to
J\cup B
.
This contradicts the assumption:
A\cup D\Perp B\given C
.
∎

Lemma I.7

(Right Weak Union)
.

A\Perp B\cup D\given C\implies A\Perp B\given D\cup C.

Proof.

Follow the same steps as in Left Weak Union
I.6
,
but this time get a contradiction with:

A\Perp B\cup D\given C
.
Then again we can assume that
\pi
contains a node in
D\setminus C
.
Then consider the shortest sub-walk
\tilde{\pi}
in
\pi
from
v\in A
to a node
u\in D\setminus C
.
This means that
\tilde{\pi}
does not contain any collider in
D\setminus C
,
so they are all in
C
. So
\tilde{\pi}
is
C
-
\sigma
-open walk from
A
to
J\cup B\cup D
.
This contradicts the assumption:
A\Perp B\cup D\given C
.
∎

Lemma I.8

(Left Contraction)
.

(A\Perp B\given D\cup C)\land(D\Perp B\given C)\implies A\cup D\Perp B\given C.

Proof.

Lets assume the contrary:
A\cup D\nPerp B\given C
.
Then there exists a shortest
C
-
\sigma
-open walk
\pi

from a node
v
in
A\cup D
to a node
w
in
J\cup B
in
\mathbf{G}
.
So every collider of
\pi
lies in
C
and every non-collider lies not in
C
and
v
is
the only node of
\pi
that lies in
(A\cup D)\setminus C
(otherwise
\pi
could be shortend).

Also
v
cannot lie in
D\setminus C
as it would contradict the assumption:
D\Perp B\given C
.
Thus
v\in A\setminus C
and
\pi
is a walk from
A
to
J\cup B
whose colliders all lie in
C\subseteq D\cup C

and all non-collider outside of
D\cup C
.
But this contradicts the other assumption:
A\Perp B\given D\cup C
.
∎

Lemma I.9

(Right Contraction)
.

(A\Perp B\given D\cup C)\land(A\Perp D\given C)\implies A\Perp B\cup D\given C.

Proof.

Lets assume the contrary:
A\nPerp B\cup D\given C
.
Then there exists a shortest
C
-
\sigma
-open walk
\pi

from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J\cup B\cup D
in
\mathbf{G}
.
So every collider of
\pi
lies in
C
and every non-collider outside
C
and
w
is
the only node of
\pi
that lies in
(J\cup B\cup D)\setminus C
(otherwise
\pi
could be shortend).

Also
w
cannot lie in
D\setminus C
as it would contradict the assumption:
A\Perp D\given C
.
Thus
w\in(J\cup B)\setminus C
and
\pi
is a walk from
A
to
J\cup B
whose colliders all lie in
C\subseteq D\cup C

and all non-colliders outside of
D\cup C
.
But this contradicts the other assumption:
A\Perp B\given D\cup C
.
∎

Lemma I.10

(Right Cross Contraction)
.

(A\Perp B\given D\cup C)\land(D\Perp A\given C)\implies A\Perp B\cup D\given C.

Proof.

Verbatim the same as Right Contraction
I.9
,
only the first contradiction is with:
D\Perp A\given C
.
∎

Lemma I.11

(Flipped Left Cross Contraction)
.

(A\Perp B\given D\cup C)\land(B\Perp D\given C)\implies B\Perp A\cup D\given C.

Proof.

Lets assume the contrary:
B\nPerp A\cup D\given C
.
Then there exists a shortest
C
-
\sigma
-open walk
\pi

from a node
v
in
B
to a node
w
in
J\cup A\cup D
in
\mathbf{G}
.
So every collider of
\pi
lies in
C
and every non-collider outside
C
and
w
is
the only node of
\pi
that lies in
(J\cup A\cup D)\setminus C
(otherwise
\pi
could be shortend).

Also
w
cannot lie in
(J\cup D)\setminus C
as it would contradict the assumption:
B\Perp D\given C
.
Thus
w\in A\setminus C
and the walk
\pi
(in reverse direction) is a walk from
A
to
B
whose colliders all lie in

C\subseteq D\cup C

and all non-colliders outside of
D\cup C
.
But this contradicts the other assumption:
A\Perp B\given D\cup C
.
∎

I.2
Further Separoid Rules for Sigma-Separation

Lemma I.12

(Left Composition)
.

(A\Perp B\given C)\land(D\Perp B\given C)\implies A\cup D\Perp B\given C.

Proof.

Let
\pi
be a walk from a node
v
in
A\cup D
to a node
w
in
J\cup B
.
If
v\in A
then
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
by assumption:
A\Perp B\given C
.
If
v\in D
then
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
by assumption:
D\Perp B\given C
.
∎

Lemma I.13

(Right Composition)
.

(A\Perp B\given C)\land(A\Perp D\given C)\implies A\Perp B\cup D\given C.

Proof.

Let
\pi
be a walk from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J\cup B\cup D
.
If
w\in J\cup B
then
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
by assumption:
A\Perp B\given C
.
If
w\in J\cup D
then
\pi
is
\sigma
-blocked by
C
by assumption:
A\Perp D\given C
.
∎

Lemma I.14

(Left Intersection)
.

Assume that
A\cap D=\emptyset
, then:

(A\Perp B\given D\cup C)\land(D\Perp B\given A\cup C)\implies A\cup D\Perp B\given C.

Proof.

Lets assume the contrary:
A\cup D\nPerp B\given C
.
Then there exists a shortest
C
-
\sigma
-open walk
\pi

from a node
v
in
A\cup D
to a node
w
in
J\cup B
in
\mathbf{G}
.
So every collider of
\pi
lies in
C
and every non-collider outside
C
and
v
is
the only node of
\pi
that lies in
(A\cup D)\setminus C
(otherwise
\pi
could be shortend).

If
v\in A
then by the disjointness of
A
and
D
we have that
v\notin D
.
Then
\pi
is a walk from
A
to
J\cup B
whose colliders lie in
C\subseteq D\cup C

and all non-colliders outside of
(D\setminus C)\cup C=D\cup C
. This contradicts the assumption:
A\Perp B\given D\cup C
.

If
v\in D
then similarly we get a contradiction:
D\Perp B\given A\cup C

∎

Lemma I.15

(Right Intersection)
.

Assume that
B\cap D=\emptyset
, then:

(A\Perp B\given D\cup C)\land(A\Perp D\given B\cup C)\implies A\Perp B\cup D\given C.

Proof.

Lets assume the contrary:
A\nPerp B\cup D\given C
.
Then there exists a shortest
C
-
\sigma
-open walk
\pi

from a node
v
in
A
to a node
w
in
J\cup B\cup D
in
\mathbf{G}
.
So every collider of
\pi
lies in
C
and every non-collider outside
C
and
w
is
the only node of
\pi
that lies in
(J\cup B\cup D)\setminus C
(otherwise
\pi
could be shortend).

If
w\notin B
then
w\in J\cup D
. In this case
\pi
is a walk from
A
to
J\cup D
where every collider lies in

C\subseteq B\cup C
and all non-colliders are outside of
(B\setminus C)\cup C=B\cup C
.
So
\pi
is a
(B\cup C)
-
\sigma
-open walk from
A
to
J\cup D
.
This contradicts the assumption:
A\Perp D\given B\cup C
.

If
w\notin D
then
w\in J\cup B
. In this case
\pi
is a walk from
A
to
J\cup B
where every collider lies in

C\subseteq D\cup C
and all non-colliders are outside of
D\cup C
.
So
\pi
is a
(D\cup C)
-
\sigma
-open walk from
A
to
J\cup D
.
This contradicts the assumption:
A\Perp B\given D\cup C
.

Since
B\cap D=\emptyset
there are no other cases (
B^{\mathsf{c}}\cup D^{\mathsf{c}}=J\cup V
) and we are done.
∎

I.3
Derived Separoid Rules for Sigma-Separation

Lemma I.16

(Restricted Symmetry)
.

(A\Perp B\given C)\land(B\Perp\emptyset\given C)\implies B\Perp A\given C.

Proof.

Follows from Flipped Left Cross Contraction
I.11
with
D=\emptyset
.
∎

Lemma I.17

(
J
-Restricted Symmetry)
.

A\Perp B\given C\cup J\implies B\Perp A\given C\cup J.

Proof.

Follows from Restricted Symmetry
I.16
and
J
-Restricted Right Redundancy
I.2
.
∎

Lemma I.18

(Symmetry)
.

If
J=\emptyset
then we have:

A\Perp B\given C\implies B\Perp A\given C.

Proof.

Follows directly from
J
-Restricted Symmetry
I.17
.
∎

Lemma I.19

(More Redundancies)
.

A\Perp B\given C\iff(A\setminus C)\Perp(B\setminus C)\given C\iff A\cup C\Perp J\cup B\cup C\given C.

I.4
Symmetrized Sigma-Separation

Let
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))=(J,V,E,L)
be a CDMG and let:

\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet)):=\left(\emptyset,J\,\dot{\cup}\,V,E,L\,\dot{\cup}\,L_{\bullet}\right),

with:

L_{\bullet}:=\left\{j_{1}\mathrel{\hbox to13.4pt{\vbox to0.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower 1.95277pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 2.98 L 13.01 2.98}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{3.59995pt}{2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 0 C 3.51 0.28 1.11 1.11 -0.55 2.08 L -0.55 -2.08 C 1.11 -1.11 3.51 -0.28 4.98 0}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{9.40007pt}{2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 4.98 0 C 3.51 0.28 1.11 1.11 -0.55 2.08 L -0.55 -2.08 C 1.11 -1.11 3.51 -0.28 4.98 0}{stroke:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}j_{2}\,|\,j_{1},j_{2}\in J,j_{1}\neq j_{2}\right\},

the directed mixed graph that arised from
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))
by connecting every two nodes from
J
with bi-directed edges.

For any subsets
A,B,C\subseteq J\cup V
we then have the ternary relation:

A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))}^{\sigma}B\given C,

given by
\sigma
-separation in the DMG
\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))
.

On the other hand, we can define the
symmetrized
\sigma
-separation
in the graph
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))
as follows:

A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma\lor}B\given C\qquad:\iff\qquad A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}B\given C\quad\lor\quad B\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}A\given C.

With these notations we then get:

Lemma I.20
.

Let
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))=(J,V,E,L)
be a CDMG and
A,B,C\subseteq J\cup V
any subsets.
We then have the equivalence:

A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma\lor}B\given C\qquad\iff\qquad A\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))}^{\sigma}B\given C.

Proof.

A\nPerp_{\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))}^{\sigma}B\given C
.

Since
\pi
was assumed to be a shortest
C
-open walk from
A
to
B
in
\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))
we know that

j_{1},j_{2}\notin C
and that
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
don’t have further bi-directed edges from
L_{\bullet}
.
So they are
C
-open walks in
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))
.
\pi_{1}
witnesses:
A\nPerp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}B\given C
,
and (the reverse of)
\pi_{2}
witnesses:
B\nPerp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}A\given C
. This shows “
\implies
”.

“
\Longleftarrow
”: Now assume:

A\nPerp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}B\given C\quad\land\quad B\nPerp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma}A\given C.

, resp., shortest

-open walks in

\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))
with

,

,

and
w_{2}\in J\cup A
.
If either

or

then
\pi_{1}
,
\pi_{2}
, resp., is a
C
-open walk in
\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))
,
and we are done. So we can assume:
w_{1},w_{2}\in J
. Then we can consider the following walk
\pi
in

\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))

given by connecting
\pi_{1}
and (the reverse of)
\pi_{2}
with a bi-directed edge.
Since
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
are non-colliders not in
C
the walk
\pi
is
C
-open in
\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))
,
which implies:

A\nPerp_{\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))}^{\sigma}B\given C.

This shows “
\Longleftarrow
”.
∎

Remark I.21
.

Note that
\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}^{\sigma\lor}
and
\Perp_{\mathbf{G}(V\,\dot{\cup}\,J|\doit(\bullet))}^{\sigma}
each form
a (symmetric)
\emptyset
-
\emptyset
-separoid by Theorems

5.11
and
A.17
.

Appendix J
Proofs - Global Markov Property

The proof of the global Markov property follows similar arguments as used in
[
LDLL90
,
Ver93
,
Ric03
,
FM17
,
FM18
,
RERS17
]
,
namely chaining the separoid rules together in an inductive way.
The main difference here is that we never rely on the Symmetry property but instead use
the left and right versions of the separoid rules separately.

Theorem J.1

(Global Markov property for causal Bayesian networks)
.

Consider a causal Bayesian network
\mathbf{M}(V|\doit(J))
with CDAG
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))

and joint Markov kernel
\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))
.
Then for all
A,B,C\subseteq J\cup V
(not-necessarily disjoint) we have the implication:

A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}B\given C\qquad\implies\qquad X_{A}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}.

If one wants to make the implicit dependence on
J
more explicit one can equivalently also write:

A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C\qquad\implies\qquad X_{A}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{J},X_{B}\given X_{C}.

Proof.

We do induction by
\#V
.

0.) Induction start:
V=\emptyset
. This means that
A,B,C\subseteq J
.
The assumption:

A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C,

implies that we must have that
A\subseteq C
. Otherwise a trivial walk from
A\subseteq J
to
J\cup B
would be
C
-open.
Since
A,B,C\subseteq J
we have the factorization:

\mathbf{P}(X_{A},X_{B},X_{C}|\doit(X_{J}))=\underbrace{\bigotimes_{w\in A}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(X_{w}|X_{w})}_{=:\mathbf{Q}(X_{A}|X_{C})}\otimes\underbrace{\bigotimes_{w\in B}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(X_{w}|X_{w})\otimes\bigotimes_{w\in C}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(X_{w}|X_{w})}_{=\mathbf{P}(X_{B},X_{C}|\doit(X_{J}))}.

Because
A\subseteq C
the Markov kernel
\mathbf{Q}(X_{A}|X_{C}):=\bigotimes_{w\in A}{\boldsymbol{\delta}}(X_{w}|X_{w})

really is a Markov kernel from
\mathcal{X}_{C}\dashrightarrow\mathcal{X}_{A}
.
This already shows:

X_{A}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}|X_{C}.

(IND): Induction assumption:
The global Markov property holds for all causal Bayesian networks
(with input variables) with
\#V<n
(and arbitrary
J
).

1.) Now assume:
\#V=n>0
and
A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C
.

Since
G
is acyclic we can find a topological order
<
for
G
where the elements of
J
are ordered first.
Let
v\in V
be its last element, which is thus childless.

Note that, since
\mathrm{Ch}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)=\emptyset
, the marginalization
\mathbf{G}(V\setminus\{v\}|\doit(J))
has no bi-directed edges and thus induces again
a causal Bayesian network without latent variables with
\#V^{\setminus\{v\}}=n-1<n
.

Furthermore, we have the factorization:

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}_{v}(X_{v}|\doit(X_{\mathrm{Pa}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)}))\otimes\underbrace{\bigotimes_{w\in\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)\setminus J}\mathbf{P}_{w}(X_{w}|\doit(X_{\mathrm{Pa}^{\mathbf{G}}(w)}))}_{\mathbf{P}(X_{\mathrm{Pred}_{<}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)\setminus J}|\doit(X_{J}))},

where
\mathrm{Pred}_{<}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)=J\,\dot{\cup}\,V\setminus\{v\}
is the set of predesessors of
\{v\}
.
This factorization implies that we already have the conditional independence:

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)}\given X_{D},

where we put
D:=\mathrm{Pa}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)
.

In the following we will distinguish between 4 cases:

A.)

v\in A\setminus C
,

B.)

v\in B\setminus C
,

C.)

v\in C
,

D.)

v\notin A\cup J\cup B\cup C
,

Note that
v\in V
, thus
v\notin J
, which shows that the above cover all possible cases.

Further note that:

A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C,

implies that:

A\cap(J\cup B)\subseteq C.

Otherwise a trivial walk from
A
to
J\cup B
would be
C
-open.
This shows that
A\setminus C
,
(J\cup B)\setminus C
and
C
are pairwise disjoint.

Case D.):
v\notin A\cup J\cup B\cup C
. Then we can marginalize out
v
and use the equivalence:

A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C\quad\iff\quad A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V\setminus\{v\}|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C.

With
\#V^{\setminus\{v\}}<n
and induction (IND) we then get:

X_{A}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}.

This shows the claim in case D.

Case A.):
v\in A\setminus C
. Then we can write:

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(A\cap C)\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\},

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(B\cap C),

with some disjoint
A^{\prime}\subseteq A\setminus C
and
B^{\prime}\subseteq B\setminus C
.
We then have the implications:

\displaystyle A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:r-dec}}}

\displaystyle A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:l-dec}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{marginalization, }v\notin A^{\prime}\cup J\cup B^{\prime}\cup C}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V\setminus\{v\}|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{induction (IND)}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{C}.

(#1)

On the other hand we have with
D=\mathrm{Pa}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)
:

\displaystyle A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:r-dec}, }B^{\prime}\subseteq B}

\displaystyle A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Weak Union \ref{sep:sig:l-uni}, }A=A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(A\cap C)\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}}

\displaystyle\{v\}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C.

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{(*),\text{ see below}}

\displaystyle D\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{marginalization, }v\notin D\cup J\cup B^{\prime}\cup A^{\prime}\cup C}

\displaystyle D\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V\setminus\{v\}|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{induction (IND)}}

\displaystyle X_{D}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle X_{D}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}.

(#2)

(*) holds since every
(A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C)
-open walk
w\mathrel{\hbox to13.4pt{\vbox to4.6pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower-0.14722pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{}{}{{}}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.89 2.98 L 15.09 2.98}{fill:none} {{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{-1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{-1.0}{2.09184pt}{2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.76 1.59 L 0 0 M 0 3.18 L 0 0 M -2.76 1.59 L 0 0 M -2.76 -1.59 L 0 0 M 0 -3.18 L 0 0 M 2.76 -1.59 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}{{}{{}}{}{}{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{10.90817pt}{2.15277pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-dasharray,stroke-dashoffset} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-linecap} \lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 2.76 1.59 L 0 0 M 0 3.18 L 0 0 M -2.76 1.59 L 0 0 M -2.76 -1.59 L 0 0 M 0 -3.18 L 0 0 M 2.76 -1.59 L 0 0}{fill:none} \lxSVG@closescope \lxSVG@closescope }}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\cdots
from a
w\in D=\mathrm{Pa}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)
to
J\cup B^{\prime}

extends to an
(A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C)
-open walk from
v
to
J\cup B^{\prime}
via
v\mathrel{\hbox to13.4pt{\vbox to0.4pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.2pt\lower 1.95277pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{{}}{}

, as
w
stays a
non-collider in the extended walk (not in
A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C
) and
v\notin A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C
.

As discussed above we also already have the conditional independence:

X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)}\given X_{D}.

With this and
A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C\subseteq\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)
we get the implications:

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)}\given X_{D}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:r-dec}}}

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{A^{\prime}},X_{B^{\prime}},X_{C}\given X_{D}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Weak Union \ref{sep:tci:r-uni}}}

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C},X_{D}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Contraction \ref{sep:tci:l-con}, }\eqref{eqn:bla}}

\displaystyle X_{v},X_{D}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:l-dec}}}

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Contraction \ref{sep:tci:l-con}, }\eqref{eqn:bli}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{X_{J}\text{-Inverted Right Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:inv-r-dec}}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{J},X_{B^{\prime}},X_{C}\given X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decompositon \ref{sep:tci:r-dec}, }B\subseteq B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}.

(#3)

By Extended Left Redundancy
E.3
we have:

X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v},X_{C}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v},X_{C}.

With this we get the implications:

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v},X_{C}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v},X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Contraction \ref{sep:tci:l-con}, }\eqref{eqn:bloop}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v},X_{A^{\prime}},X_{v},X_{C}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:l-dec}, }A\subseteq A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle X_{A}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}.

This shows the claim in case A.

Case B.):
v\in B\setminus C
. Then we can write:

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(A\cap C),

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(B\cap C)\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\},

with some disjoint
A^{\prime}\subseteq A\setminus C
and
B^{\prime}\subseteq B\setminus C
.

We then have the implications:

\displaystyle A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:l-dec}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:r-dec}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{marginalization, }v\notin A^{\prime}\cup J\cup B^{\prime}\cup C}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V\setminus\{v\}|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{induction (IND)}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}}\given X_{C}.

(#1’)

Again with
D=\mathrm{Pa}^{\mathbf{G}}(v)
we get:

\displaystyle A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:l-dec}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:r-dec}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\cup\{v\}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Weak Union \ref{sep:sig:r-uni}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup\{v\}\given B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{(\bullet),\text{ see below}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup D\given B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{induction (IND)}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{D}\given X_{B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{D}\given X_{B^{\prime}},X_{C}.

(#2’)

(\bullet)
holds since every
(B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C)
-open walk

.
Note again that
w
stays a non-collider in the extended walk (outside of
B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C
) and
v\notin B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C
.

As before we will use the following conditional independence:

X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)}\given X_{D}.

With this and
A^{\prime}\cup J\cup B^{\prime}\cup C\subseteq\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)
we get the implications:

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{\mathrm{Pred}^{\mathbf{G}}_{<}(v)}\given X_{D}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:r-dec}}}

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{A^{\prime}},X_{B^{\prime}},X_{C}\given X_{D}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Weak Union \ref{sep:tci:r-uni}}}

\displaystyle X_{v}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{A^{\prime}}\given X_{B^{\prime}},X_{C},X_{D}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Flipped Left Cross Contraction \ref{sep:tci:flc-con}, }\eqref{eqn:bla'}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{D},X_{v}\given X_{B^{\prime}},X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:r-dec}}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{v}\given X_{B^{\prime}},X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Contraction \ref{sep:tci:r-con}, }\eqref{eqn:bli'}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B^{\prime}},X_{v}\given X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{$X_{J}$-Inverted Right Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:inv-r-dec}}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{J},X_{B^{\prime}},X_{v},X_{C}\given X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:r-dec}, }B\subseteq B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}.

(#3’)

By Extended Left Redundancy
E.3
we have:

X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}.

With this we get the implications:

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Contraction \ref{sep:tci:l-con}, }\eqref{eqn:bloop'}}

\displaystyle X_{A^{\prime}},X_{A^{\prime}},X_{C}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}.

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Decomposition \ref{sep:tci:l-dec}, }A\subseteq A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,C}

\displaystyle X_{A}\Indep_{\mathbf{P}(X_{V}|\doit(X_{J}))}X_{B}\given X_{C}.

This shows the claim in case B.

Case C.):
v\in C
.
Then we can write:

\displaystyle A

\displaystyle=A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(A\cap C),

\displaystyle B

\displaystyle=B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,(B\cap C),

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle=C^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\},

with some pairwise disjoint
A^{\prime}\subseteq A\setminus C
,

B^{\prime}\subseteq B\setminus C
and

C^{\prime}\subseteq C
.

We then get the implications.

\displaystyle A\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Left Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:l-dec}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{\text{Right Decomposition \ref{sep:sig:r-dec}}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{C=C^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}}

\displaystyle A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}

We now claim that:

A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}

implies that one of the following statements holds:

A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C^{\prime}\qquad\lor\qquad A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup(B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\})\given C^{\prime}.

Assume the contrary:

A^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}\nPerp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C^{\prime}\qquad\land\qquad A^{\prime}\nPerp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup(B^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\})\given C^{\prime}.

So there exist shortest
C^{\prime}
-open walks
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
in
\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))
:

So all colliders of
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are in
C^{\prime}
and all non-colliders outside of
C^{\prime}
.
Since we consider shortest walks and
v\notin C^{\prime}
at most an end node of
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
could be equal to
v
.
Otherwise one could shorten the walk.

Then note that
v\notin A^{\prime}
and
v\notin J\cup B^{\prime}
, thus:
u_{k}\neq v
and
w_{0}\neq v
.

If now
\pi_{i}
does not contain
v
as an (end) node, then
\pi_{i}
would be
(C^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\})
-open,
which is a contradiction to the assumption:

A^{\prime}\Perp^{\sigma}_{\mathbf{G}(V|\doit(J))}J\cup B^{\prime}\given C^{\prime}\,\dot{\cup}\,\{v\}.

So we can assume that the other end nodes equal
v
, i.e.:
u_{0}=v
and
w_{m}=v
.

Furthermore, both
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are non-trivial walks, since
u_{0}\neq u_{k}
and
w_{0}\neq w_{m}
.
Since
v
is childless and
k,m\geq 1
we have that the
\pi_{i}
are of the forms:
